```suggestion
#' - An integer or double vector holds 1D locations. `x` is treated as its
#'   equivalent flattened array, so location `4` in a 2 x 3 matrix is row 2,
#'   column 2. Locations follow the usual R rules: negative values drop
#'   elements, zero is ignored, duplicates repeat elements, and `NA` gives a
#'   missing value.
```

 27 files changed, 2365 insertions(+), 611 deletions(-)
     1	#' Extract values from an array
     2	#'
     3	#' `rray_extract()` selects values from `x` by flat position or by coordinate
     4	#' points. The result is always a one-dimensional array with no names.
     5	#'
     6	#' @details
     7	#' The type and shape of `i` decide how it is used.
     8	#'
     9	#' - An integer or double vector holds flat positions. `x` is treated as its
    10	#'   column-major storage vector, so position `4` in a 2 x 3 matrix is row 2,
    11	#'   column 2. Positions follow the usual R rules: negative values drop
    12	#'   elements, zero is ignored, duplicates repeat elements, and `NA` gives a
    13	#'   missing value.
    14	#'
    15	#' - A logical vector is a flat mask. It must be size 1 or the size of `x`. A
    16	#'   logical array is also a mask if it has the same dimensions as `x`, so
    17	#'   `rray_extract(x, x > 5)` works.
    18	#'
    19	#' - An integer or double matrix holds coordinate points. Each row selects one
    20	#'   element, and there must be one column per axis of `x`. Coordinates must be
    21	#'   positive and within the dimension of their axis. `NA` gives a missing
    22	#'   value.
    23	#'
    24	#' For raw arrays the missing value is `as.raw(0)`, and for list arrays it is
    25	#' `NULL`.
    26	#'
    27	#' @param x A bare array or vector.
    28	#'
    29	#' @param i A numeric vector of flat positions, a logical mask, or a numeric
    30	#'   matrix of coordinate points.
    31	#'
    32	#' @returns
    33	#' A one-dimensional array with the same storage type as `x`.
    34	#'
    35	#' @export
    36	#' @examples
    37	#' x <- array(1:24, c(2L, 3L, 4L))
    38	#'
    39	#' # Flat positions, in column-major order
    40	#' rray_extract(x, c(1, 4, 24))
    41	#'
    42	#' # Negative positions drop elements
    43	#' rray_extract(x, -(1:20))
    44	#'
    45	#' # A logical mask with the same dimensions as `x`
    46	#' rray_extract(x, x %% 5 == 0)
    47	#'
    48	#' # One row per point, one column per axis
    49	#' points <- rbind(
    50	#'   c(1, 1, 1),
    51	#'   c(2, 3, 4),
    52	#'   c(1, 2, 3)
    53	#' )
    54	#' rray_extract(x, points)
    55	rray_extract <- function(x, i) {
    56	  .Call(ffi_rray_extract, x, i, environment())
    57	}

    53	struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_subscript(
    54	  r_obj* i,
    55	  const int* v_dimensions,
    56	  int dimensionality,
    57	  struct rray_arg* i_arg,
    58	  struct r_lazy error_call
    59	) {
    60	  check_unclassed(i, i_arg, error_call);
    61	
    62	  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
    63	
    64	  switch (r_typeof(i)) {
    65	  case R_TYPE_logical:
    66	    return rray_as_extract_mask(
    67	      i,
    68	      v_dimensions,
    69	      dimensionality,
    70	      size,
    71	      i_arg,
    72	      error_call
    73	    );
    74	  case R_TYPE_integer:
    75	  case R_TYPE_double: {
    76	    r_obj* i_dimensions = r_dim(i);
    77	    const int i_dimensionality = i_dimensions == r_null
    78	      ? 1
    79	      : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(i_dimensions);
    80	
    81	    switch (i_dimensionality) {
    82	    case 1:
    83	      return rray_as_extract_positions(i, size, i_arg, error_call);
    84	    case 2:
    85	      return rray_as_extract_points(
    86	        i,
    87	        v_dimensions,
    88	        dimensionality,
    89	        i_arg,
    90	        error_call
    91	      );
    92	    default:
    93	      r_abort_lazy_call(
    94	        error_call,
    95	        "Numeric %s must be a vector or a matrix, not an array with "
    96	        "%d dimensions.",
    97	        rray_arg_format(i_arg),
    98	        i_dimensionality
    99	      );
   100	    }
   101	  }
   102	  default:
   103	    r_abort_lazy_call(
   104	      error_call,
   105	      "%s must be logical, integer, or double, not %s.",
   106	      rray_arg_format(i_arg),
   107	      r_obj_type_friendly(i)
   108	    );
   109	  }
   110	}

   112	static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_mask(
   113	  r_obj* i,
   114	  const int* v_dimensions,
   115	  int dimensionality,
   116	  r_ssize size,

   150	      i_size
   151	    );
   152	  }

   156	    .kind = RRAY_EXTRACT_SUBSCRIPT_KIND_mask,
   157	    .size = rray_mask_size(i, size)

    76	static inline r_ssize rray_position_offset_int(int position) {
    77	  return position == r_globals.na_int ? -1 : (r_ssize) position - 1;
    78	}
    79	
    80	static inline r_ssize rray_position_offset_dbl(double position) {
    81	  return isnan(position) ? -1 : (r_ssize) position - 1;
    82	}
    83	
    84	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_int(
    85	  const int* v_point,
    86	  r_ssize size,
    87	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
    88	  int dimensionality
    89	) {
    90	  r_ssize out = 0;
    91	
    92	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
    93	    const int coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
    94	
    95	    if (coordinate == r_globals.na_int) {
    96	      return -1;
    97	    }
    98	
    99	    out += (r_ssize) (coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
   100	  }
   101	
   102	  return out;
   103	}
   104	
   105	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_dbl(
   106	  const double* v_point,
   107	  r_ssize size,
   108	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
   109	  int dimensionality
   110	) {
   111	  r_ssize out = 0;
   112	
   113	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
   114	    const double coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
   115	
   116	    if (isnan(coordinate)) {
   117	      return -1;
   118	    }
   119	
   120	    out += ((r_ssize) coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
   121	  }
   122	
   123	  return out;
   124	}

   126	#define RRAY_EXTRACT_POSITIONS_LOOP(                                           \
   127	  POKE,                                                                        \
   128	  MISSING,                                                                     \
   129	  I_CTYPE,                                                                     \
   130	  I_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
   131	  OFFSET                                                                       \
   132	)                                                                              \
   133	  do {                                                                         \
   134	    const I_CTYPE* v_i = I_CONST_DEREF(subscript.i);                           \
   135	                                                                               \
   136	    for (r_ssize j = 0; j < subscript.size; ++j) {                             \
   137	      const r_ssize offset = OFFSET(v_i[j]);                                   \
   138	      POKE(out, j, offset == -1 ? MISSING : v_x[offset]);                      \
   139	    }                                                                          \
   140	  } while (0)
   141	
   142	#define RRAY_EXTRACT_ATOMIC_MASK_LOOP(POKE, MISSING)                           \
   143	  do {                                                                         \
   144	    const int* v_i = r_lgl_cbegin(subscript.i);                                \
   145	    const r_ssize i_step = r_length(subscript.i) == 1 ? 0 : 1;                 \
   146	                                                                               \
   147	    r_ssize j = 0;                                                             \
   148	                                                                               \
   149	    for (r_ssize offset = 0; j < subscript.size; ++offset) {                   \

   152	      j += elt != 0;                                                           \
   153	    }                                                                          \
   154	  } while (0)

   156	#define RRAY_EXTRACT_BARRIER_MASK_LOOP(POKE, MISSING)                          \

   158	    const int* v_i = r_lgl_cbegin(subscript.i);                                \

   160	    const r_ssize x_size = r_length(x);                                        \

   167	      if (elt == 0) {                                                          \

   171	      POKE(out, j, elt == r_globals.na_lgl ? MISSING : v_x[offset]);           \
   172	      ++j;                                                                     \
   173	    }                                                                          \
   174	  } while (0)

Pulled all 27 review threads on PR #87. I'll go through them in order, with one commit and push per thread.

Bash: git status -sb | head -2 && git diff main...HEAD -- src/strided-iterator.h | grep -n position… 
git status -sb | head -2 && git diff main...HEAD -- src/strided-iterator.h | grep -n position; cat ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/feedback_no_section_comments_in_tests.md
## feature/extract...origin/feature/extract
---
name: Test section comments only separate functions
description: In test files, `# ----` headers separate one function's tests from another's, never subgroups within one function
type: feedback
---

In `tests/testthat/`, use a `# ----` divider plus the function name only to
separate the tests for one function from the tests for another in the same file.
Never use them to label subgroups within a single function's tests (e.g.
`# -- Validation --`).

The format is an 80 character rule, then the function with `()`:

```r
# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# rray_broadcast_common()
```

**Why:** A file covering two functions needs the boundary marked, which is what
vctrs does and what `test-dimensions.R` and `test-wrapper.R` already do. Within
one function, the `test_that()` descriptions are enough.

**How to apply:** When a `test-{name}.R` file grows to cover a second function,
add a header above the first test of each function, including the one already at
the top of the file. Under a header, name tests plainly ("single input works"),
without repeating the function in every description, but keep names unique
within the file so snapshots don't collide.

     3	#' `rray_extract()` selects values from `x` by 1D location or by coordinate
     4	#' point. The result is always a 1D array.
     5	#'
     6	#' @details
     7	#' The type and shape of `i` decide how it is used:
     8	#'
     9	#' - An integer or double vector holds 1D locations. `x` is treated as its
    10	#'   equivalent flattened array, so location `4` in a 2 x 3 matrix is row 2,
    11	#'   column 2. Locations follow the usual R rules: negative values drop
    12	#'   elements, zero is ignored, duplicates repeat elements, and `NA` gives a
    13	#'   missing value.
    14	#'
    15	#' - A logical vector must be size 1 or the size of `x`. A logical array must
    16	#'   have the same dimensions as `x`, so `rray_extract(x, x > 5)` works. When
    17	#'   `TRUE`, the corresponding value in `x` is returned.
    18	#'
    19	#' - An integer or double matrix holds coordinate points. Each row selects one
    20	#'   element, and there must be one column per axis of `x`. Coordinates must be
    21	#'   positive and within the dimension of their axis. `NA` at any position
    22	#'   within the row generates a missing value.
    23	#'
    24	#' For raw arrays the missing value is `as.raw(0)`, and for list arrays it is
    25	#' `NULL`.
    26	#'
    27	#' @param x An array.
    28	#'
    29	#' @param i A numeric vector of locations, a logical vector, a logical array
    30	#'   with the same dimensions as `x`, or a numeric matrix of coordinate points.
    31	#'
    32	#' @returns
    33	#' A one-dimensional array. Names are always dropped.
    34	#'
    35	#' @export

Read src/extract-subscript.c 
112	static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_mask(
113	  r_obj* i,
114	  const int* v_dimensions,
115	  int dimensionality,
116	  r_ssize size,
117	  struct rray_arg* i_arg,
118	  struct r_lazy error_call
119	) {
120	  r_obj* i_dimensions = r_dim(i);
121	  const int i_dimensionality = i_dimensions == r_null
122	    ? 1
123	    : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(i_dimensions);
124	
125	  if (i_dimensionality != 1) {
126	    const bool equal = rray_dimensions_are_equal(
127	      r_int_cbegin(i_dimensions),
128	      i_dimensionality,
129	      v_dimensions,
130	      dimensionality
131	    );
132	
133	    if (!equal) {
134	      r_abort_lazy_call(
135	        error_call,
136	        "Logical %s must be a vector or have the same dimensions as `x`.",
137	        rray_arg_format(i_arg)
138	      );
139	    }
140	  }
141	
142	  const r_ssize i_size = r_length(i);
143	
144	  if (i_size != 1 && i_size != size) {
145	    r_abort_lazy_call(
146	      error_call,
147	      "Logical %s must be size 1 or %" R_PRI_SSIZE ", not %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
148	      rray_arg_format(i_arg),
149	      size,
150	      i_size
151	    );
152	  }
153	
154	  return (struct rray_extract_subscript){
155	    .i = i,
156	    .kind = RRAY_EXTRACT_SUBSCRIPT_KIND_mask,
157	    .size = rray_mask_size(i, size)
158	  };
159	}

Thinking

Confirmed: rray_as_extract_complement() skips zero entries, so a zero in a negative subscript drops nothing—already covered by two existing tests (c(-2, 0, -2) and c(-1, 0, -6)). I'll reply to thread 14 with this answer and leave it open.

Bash: gh api graphql -f query='mutation($t:ID!,$b:String!){addPullRequestReviewThreadReply(input:{p… 
gh api graphql -f query='mutation($t:ID!,$b:String!){addPullRequestReviewThreadReply(input:{pullRequestReviewThreadId:$t,body:$b}){comment{id}}}' -f t=PRRT_kwDOUHAoJs6mCrNC -f b='Yes. `rray_as_extract_complement()` only clears a slot when `elt != 0`, so zeros mixed in with negatives drop nothing and the mask is built as if they were not there. No separate zero pass is needed on that path.

Covered by `rray_as_extract_subscript(c(-2, 0, -2), 3L)` in `test-extract-subscript.R` and `rray_extract(x, c(-1, 0, -6))` in `test-extract.R`. No code change, leaving this open for you to resolve.'

20	r_obj* ffi_rray_as_extract_subscript(
21	  r_obj* ffi_i,
22	  r_obj* ffi_dimensions,
23	  r_obj* ffi_frame
24	) {
25	  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
26	
27	  r_obj* dimensions =
28	    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(ffi_dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));
29	
30	  const struct rray_extract_subscript subscript = rray_as_extract_subscript(
31	    ffi_i,
32	    r_int_cbegin(dimensions),
33	    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions),
34	    rray_args.i,
35	    error_call
36	  );
37	  KEEP(subscript.i);
38	
39	  const char* v_names[] = {"i", "kind", "size"};
40	  r_obj* names = KEEP(r_chr_n(v_names, 3));
41	
42	  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_list(3));
43	  r_attrib_poke_names(out, names);
44	
45	  r_list_poke(out, 0, subscript.i);
46	  r_list_poke(out, 1, r_chr(rray_extract_subscript_kind_name(subscript.kind)));
47	  r_list_poke(out, 2, r_int(r_ssize_as_integer(subscript.size)));
48	
49	  FREE(4);
50	  return out;
51	}
52	
53	struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_subscript(
54	  r_obj* i,
55	  const int* v_dimensions,
56	  int dimensionality,
57	  struct rray_arg* i_arg,
58	  struct r_lazy error_call
59	) {
60	  check_unclassed(i, i_arg, error_call);
61	
62	  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);
63	
64	  switch (r_typeof(i)) {
65	  case R_TYPE_logical:
66	    return rray_as_extract_mask(
67	      i,
68	      v_dimensions,
69	      dimensionality,
70	      size,
71	      i_arg,
72	      error_call
73	    );
74	  case R_TYPE_integer:
75	  case R_TYPE_double: {
76	    r_obj* i_dimensions = r_dim(i);
77	    const int i_dimensionality = i_dimensions == r_null
78	      ? 1
79	      : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(i_dimensions);
80	
81	    switch (i_dimensionality) {
82	    case 1:
83	      return rray_as_extract_locations(i, size, i_arg, error_call);
84	    case 2:
85	      return rray_as_extract_points(
86	        i,
87	        v_dimensions,
88	        dimensionality,
89	        i_arg,
90	        error_call
91	      );
92	    default:
93	      r_abort_lazy_call(
94	        error_call,
95	        "Numeric %s must be a vector or a matrix, not an array with "
96	        "%d dimensions.",
97	        rray_arg_format(i_arg),
98	        i_dimensionality
99	      );
100	    }
101	  }
102	  default:
103	    r_abort_lazy_call(
104	      error_call,
105	      "%s must be logical, integer, or double, not %s.",
106	      rray_arg_format(i_arg),
107	      r_obj_type_friendly(i)
108	    );
109	  }
110	}
111	
112	static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_mask(
113	  r_obj* i,
114	  const int* v_dimensions,
115	  int dimensionality,
116	  r_ssize size,
117	  struct rray_arg* i_arg,
118	  struct r_lazy error_call
119	) {
120	  r_obj* i_dimensions = r_dim(i);
121	  const int i_dimensionality = i_dimensions == r_null
122	    ? 1
123	    : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(i_dimensions);
124	
125	  if (i_dimensionality == 1) {
126	    // Check for size 1 or size `size`. Note we don't expand a single `TRUE`, we
127	    // have native support for that.
128	    const r_ssize i_size = r_length(i);
129	
130	    if (i_size != 1 && i_size != size) {
131	      r_abort_lazy_call(
132	        error_call,
133	        "Logical %s must be size 1 or %" R_PRI_SSIZE ", not %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
134	        rray_arg_format(i_arg),
135	        size,
136	        i_size
137	      );
138	    }
139	  } else {
140	    // If a logical array is provided, it must match `x` dimensions exactly. In
141	    // theory it could broadcast but likely not worth it.
142	    const bool equal = rray_dimensions_are_equal(
143	      r_int_cbegin(i_dimensions),
144	      i_dimensionality,
145	      v_dimensions,
146	      dimensionality
147	    );
148	
149	    if (!equal) {
150	      r_abort_lazy_call(
151	        error_call,
152	        "Logical array %s must have the same dimensions as `x`.",
153	        rray_arg_format(i_arg)
154	      );
155	    }
156	  }
157

I'll rewrite src/extract-subscript.c in full, then check the word diff to confirm only the intended identifiers changed.

Write src/extract-subscript.c 
#include "extract-subscript.h"

#include <math.h>

#include "dimensionality.h"
#include "dimensions.h"
#include "size.h"
#include "utils.h"

struct rray_subscript_summary {
  double min;
  double max;
  r_ssize zeros;
  bool any_missing;
  bool any_fractional;
};

#include "decl/extract-subscript-decl.h"

r_obj* ffi_rray_as_extract_subscript(
  r_obj* ffi_i,
  r_obj* ffi_dimensions,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};

  r_obj* dimensions =
    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(ffi_dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));

  const struct rray_extract_subscript subscript = rray_as_extract_subscript(
    ffi_i,
    r_int_cbegin(dimensions),
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions),
    rray_args.i,
    error_call
  );
  KEEP(subscript.index);

  const char* v_names[] = {"index", "kind", "size"};
  r_obj* names = KEEP(r_chr_n(v_names, 3));

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_list(3));
  r_attrib_poke_names(out, names);

  r_list_poke(out, 0, subscript.index);
  r_list_poke(out, 1, r_chr(rray_extract_subscript_kind_name(subscript.kind)));
  r_list_poke(out, 2, r_int(r_ssize_as_integer(subscript.size)));

  FREE(4);
  return out;
}

struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_subscript(
  r_obj* index,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  struct rray_arg* index_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(index, index_arg, error_call);

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  switch (r_typeof(index)) {
  case R_TYPE_logical:
    return rray_as_extract_mask(
      index,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      size,
      index_arg,
      error_call
    );
  case R_TYPE_integer:
  case R_TYPE_double: {
    r_obj* index_dimensions = r_dim(index);
    const int index_dimensionality = index_dimensions == r_null
      ? 1
      : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(index_dimensions);

    switch (index_dimensionality) {
    case 1:
      return rray_as_extract_locations(index, size, index_arg, error_call);
    case 2:
      return rray_as_extract_points(
        index,
        v_dimensions,
        dimensionality,
        index_arg,
        error_call
      );
    default:
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "Numeric %s must be a vector or a matrix, not an array with "
        "%d dimensions.",
        rray_arg_format(index_arg),
        index_dimensionality
      );
    }
  }
  default:
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "%s must be logical, integer, or double, not %s.",
      rray_arg_format(index_arg),
      r_obj_type_friendly(index)
    );
  }
}

static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_mask(
  r_obj* index,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  r_ssize size,
  struct rray_arg* index_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_obj* index_dimensions = r_dim(index);
  const int index_dimensionality = index_dimensions == r_null
    ? 1
    : rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(index_dimensions);

  if (index_dimensionality == 1) {
    // Check for size 1 or size `size`. Note we don't expand a single `TRUE`, we
    // have native support for that.
    const r_ssize index_size = r_length(index);

    if (index_size != 1 && index_size != size) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "Logical %s must be size 1 or %" R_PRI_SSIZE ", not %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
        rray_arg_format(index_arg),
        size,
        index_size
      );
    }
  } else {
    // If a logical array is provided, it must match `x` dimensions exactly. In
    // theory it could broadcast but likely not worth it.
    const bool equal = rray_dimensions_are_equal(
      r_int_cbegin(index_dimensions),
      index_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    );

    if (!equal) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "Logical array %s must have the same dimensions as `x`.",
        rray_arg_format(index_arg)
      );
    }
  }

  return (struct rray_extract_subscript){
    .index = index,
    .kind = RRAY_EXTRACT_SUBSCRIPT_KIND_mask,
    .size = rray_mask_size(index, size)
  };
}

static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_locations(
  r_obj* index,
  r_ssize size,
  struct rray_arg* index_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const r_ssize index_size = r_length(index);
  const struct rray_subscript_summary summary =
    rray_subscript_summarise(index, 0, index_size);

  if (summary.any_fractional) {
    stop_subscript_fractional(index_arg, error_call);
  }
  if (summary.max > size) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "%s must not contain values greater than %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
      rray_arg_format(index_arg),
      size
    );
  }
  if (summary.min < -size) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "%s must not contain values less than -%" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
      rray_arg_format(index_arg),
      size
    );
  }

  const bool any_negative = summary.min < 0;

  if (any_negative && summary.max > 0) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "%s can't mix positive and negative values.",

76	static inline r_ssize rray_location_offset_int(int location) {
77	  return location == r_globals.na_int ? -1 : (r_ssize) location - 1;
78	}
79	
80	static inline r_ssize rray_location_offset_dbl(double location) {
81	  return isnan(location) ? -1 : (r_ssize) location - 1;
82	}
83	
84	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_int(
85	  const int* v_point,
86	  r_ssize size,
87	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
88	  int dimensionality
89	) {
90	  r_ssize out = 0;
91	
92	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
93	    const int coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
94	
95	    if (coordinate == r_globals.na_int) {
96	      return -1;
97	    }
98	
99	    out += (r_ssize) (coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
100	  }
101	
102	  return out;
103	}
104	
105	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_dbl(
106	  const double* v_point,
107	  r_ssize size,
108	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
109	  int dimensionality
110	) {
111	  r_ssize out = 0;
112	
113	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
114	    const double coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
115	
116	    if (isnan(coordinate)) {
117	      return -1;
118	    }
119	
120	    out += ((r_ssize) coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
121	  }
122	
123	  return out;
124	}

I'll rename size to rows in rray_as_extract_points(), keeping its type as r_ssize since it's multiplied by the column number—using int risks overflow for large matrices where rows × columns exceeds INT_MAX.

Read src/extract-subscript.c 
228	static struct rray_extract_subscript rray_as_extract_points(
229	  r_obj* index,
230	  const int* v_dimensions,
231	  int dimensionality,
232	  struct rray_arg* index_arg,
233	  struct r_lazy error_call
234	) {
235	  const int* v_index_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(r_dim(index));
236	  const r_ssize size = v_index_dimensions[0];
237	  const int columns = v_index_dimensions[1];
238	
239	  if (columns != dimensionality) {
240	    r_abort_lazy_call(
241	      error_call,
242	      "Numeric matrix %s must have %d column%s, one for each axis of `x`, "
243	      "not %d.",
244	      rray_arg_format(index_arg),
245	      dimensionality,
246	      dimensionality == 1 ? "" : "s",
247	      columns
248	    );

255	
256	    if (summary.any_fractional) {
257	      stop_subscript_fractional(index_arg, error_call);
258	    }
259	    if (summary.min < 1) {
260	      r_abort_lazy_call(

262	        "Column %d of %s must only contain positive values or missing "

265	        rray_arg_format(index_arg)
266	      );
267	    }

273	        rray_arg_format(index_arg),

Kept it \`r_ssize\`. It gets multiplied by the column number to find where each column starts (\`axis * rows\` here, and \`column * rows\` in the point offset helpers in \`extract.c\`). 84	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_int(
85	  const int* v_point,
86	  r_ssize size,
87	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
88	  int dimensionality
89	) {
90	  r_ssize out = 0;
91	
92	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
93	    const int coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
94	
95	    if (coordinate == r_globals.na_int) {
96	      return -1;
97	    }
98	
99	    out += (r_ssize) (coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
100	  }
101	
102	  return out;
103	}

108	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
109	  int dimensionality
110	) {

118	    }
119	
120	    out += ((r_ssize) coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];

122

333	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_int(
334	  const int* v_point,
335	  r_ssize size,
336	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
337	  int dimensionality
338	) {
339	  r_ssize out = 0;
340	
341	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
342	    const int coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
343	
344	    if (coordinate == r_globals.na_int) {
345	      return -1;
346	    }
347	
348	    out += (r_ssize) (coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
349	  }
350	
351	  return out;
352	}
353	
354	static inline r_ssize rray_point_offset_dbl(
355	  const double* v_point,
356	  r_ssize size,
357	  const r_ssize* v_strides,
358	  int dimensionality
359	) {
360	  r_ssize out = 0;
361	
362	  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
363	    const double coordinate = v_point[axis * size];
364	
365	    if (isnan(coordinate)) {
366	      return -1;
367	    }
368	
369	    out += ((r_ssize) coordinate - 1) * v_strides[axis];
370	  }
371	
372	  return out;

Read ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/feedback_no_section_c… 
<system-reminder>This memory is 23 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
1	---
2	name: Test section comments only separate functions
3	description: In test files, `# ----` headers separate one function's tests from another's, never subgroups within one function
4	type: feedback
5	---
6	
7	In `tests/testthat/`, use a `# ----` divider plus the function name only to
8	separate the tests for one function from the tests for another in the same file.
9	Never use them to label subgroups within a single function's tests (e.g.
10	`# -- Validation --`).
11	
12	The format is an 80 character rule, then the function with `()`:
13	
14	```r
15	# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16	# rray_broadcast_common()
17	```
18	
19	**Why:** A file covering two functions needs the boundary marked, which is what
20	vctrs does and what `test-dimensions.R` and `test-wrapper.R` already do. Within
21	one function, the `test_that()` descriptions are enough.
22	
23	**How to apply:** When a `test-{name}.R` file grows to cover a second function,
24	add a header above the first test of each function, including the one already at
25	the top of the file. Under a header, name tests plainly ("single input works"),
26	without repeating the function in every description, but keep names unique
27	within the file so snapshots don't collide.

## feature/extract...origin/feature/extract
00be188 Drop section headers from single-function extract test files

I've handled all 27 review threads. I pushed 24 commits, and 26 threads are resolved on GitHub, each with a link to the commit that fixed it. The full test suite passes (2506 tests) and pkgdown::check_pkgdown() finds no problems. The branch is up to date with origin/feature/extract.

Still open

  • Thread 14 ("Is complement handling the zeros case automatically?"): I answered on the thread and left it open for you. Yes: rray_as_extract_complement() only clears a slot when elt != 0, so zeros mixed with negatives drop nothing. The tests for c(-2, 0, -2) and c(-1, 0, -6) already cover it.

Position to location rename (thread 9)

  • C code: KIND_locations_int/dbl, rray_as_extract_locations(), rray_location_offset_int/dbl(location) and RRAY_EXTRACT_LOCATIONS_LOOP.

  • Tests: the kind strings "locations_int"/"locations_dbl" and every test name, so the snapshot headers were regenerated too. I also renamed the flat variable in the storage-type test to locations.

  • Two uses of "position" remain in files this PR touches:

    • Your suggested doc wording for thread 5: "NA at any position within the row". That's plain English rather than the 1D-location idea, so I kept it. Say if you want it changed.
    • An existing comment in src/strided-iterator.h about the iterator's positions. It's a different concept and it isn't in this PR's changes, so I left it.

The i to index rename (threads 17 and 20)

  • I did this as one commit and resolved both threads with it.
  • rray_extract() keeps i and i_arg, as thread 20 asked. ffi_i and rray_args.i also stay, because they mirror the R argument name that shows up in error messages. Everything else now uses index, index_arg, v_index, index_size, index_step and INDEX_CTYPE, and loops use i.
  • Two changes you didn't explicitly ask for:
    • In rray_as_extract_nonzero(), the output counter k became j, since j was now free.
    • The list returned by ffi_rray_as_extract_subscript(), and the test helper new_extract_subscript(), now name that element index instead of i, to match the struct field.

Where I departed from a suggestion

  • Threads 19 and 24: both suggestions read for (int column = 0; axis < columns; ++axis). I used column all the way through.
  • Thread 25: the header comment says `index` rather than `i`, because the parameter had been renamed by then.
  • Thread 18: kept rows as r_ssize. It's multiplied by the column number to find where each column starts. A point matrix can be a long vector (more than INT_MAX elements in total), so that product could overflow as int.

Every comment you rewrote in a suggestion is reflowed to 80 characters. I also updated my memory note on test section headers: a file that tests only one function gets none.

Both builds are installed and extract tests pass. Next I'll run the benchmark script, covering int/double locations with and without NA in random and sequential order, plus a character case to test the slower r_chr_poke() path.

Write /tmp/rray4-bench/bench.R 
args <- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
lib <- args[[1]]
label <- args[[2]]

library(rray4, lib.loc = lib)

set.seed(1)

n_x <- 1e6
n_i <- 1e7

x_dbl <- array(runif(n_x), c(100L, 100L, 100L))
x_chr <- array(as.character(sample(1000L, n_x, TRUE)), c(100L, 100L, 100L))

random_int <- sample.int(n_x, n_i, replace = TRUE)
random_dbl <- as.double(random_int)
sequential_int <- rep_len(seq_len(n_x), n_i)
sequential_dbl <- as.double(sequential_int)

with_na <- function(x) {
  x[sample.int(length(x), length(x) %/% 10L)] <- NA
  x
}

random_int_na <- with_na(random_int)
random_dbl_na <- with_na(random_dbl)
sequential_int_na <- with_na(sequential_int)
sequential_dbl_na <- with_na(sequential_dbl)

random_int_chr <- random_int[seq_len(1e6)]
sequential_int_chr <- sequential_int[seq_len(1e6)]

cases <- list(
  "dbl x, random int" = list(x_dbl, random_int),
  "dbl x, random dbl" = list(x_dbl, random_dbl),
  "dbl x, random int, 10% NA" = list(x_dbl, random_int_na),
  "dbl x, random dbl, 10% NA" = list(x_dbl, random_dbl_na),
  "dbl x, sequential int" = list(x_dbl, sequential_int),
  "dbl x, sequential dbl" = list(x_dbl, sequential_dbl),
  "dbl x, sequential int, 10% NA" = list(x_dbl, sequential_int_na),
  "dbl x, sequential dbl, 10% NA" = list(x_dbl, sequential_dbl_na),
  "chr x, random int (1e6)" = list(x_chr, random_int_chr),
  "chr x, sequential int (1e6)" = list(x_chr, sequential_int_chr)
)

out <- lapply(names(cases), function(name) {
  case <- cases[[name]]
  x <- case[[1]]
  i <- case[[2]]
  result <- bench::mark(
    rray_extract(x, i),
    min_iterations = 40,
    check = FALSE,
    filter_gc = FALSE
  )
  data.frame(
    build = label,
    case = name,
    median_ms = as.numeric(result$median) * 1000
  )
})

out <- do.call(rbind, out)
write.csv(out, stdout(), row.names = FALSE)

Thinking

Both builds fluctuate between ~20.5-26.5 ms per round, so the 1.28x difference is just noise—likely from macOS shifting the process across fast/slow CPU cores. I'll instead compare the actual machine code generated for each version, which is a more reliable check.

Bash: cat src/Makevars 2>/dev/null; R CMD config CC; R CMD config --cppflags

Thinking

The int-location loop confirms the same real savings (11 vs 8 instructions per element, shorter dependency chain), so the compiler wasn't already optimizing this. I'll rerun timing with five rounds per build, tracking minimum times alongside medians for more stable results.

Bash: cd /tmp/rray4-bench && Rscript -e ' 
cd /tmp/rray4-bench && Rscript -e '
x <- read.csv("results2.csv", header = FALSE, col.names = c("round", "build", "case", "min_ms", "median_ms"))
best <- aggregate(min_ms ~ case + build, x, min)
med <- aggregate(median_ms ~ case + build, x, median)
w <- merge(
  reshape(best, idvar = "case", timevar = "build", direction = "wide"),
  reshape(med, idvar = "case", timevar = "build", direction = "wide")
)
w <- w[match(unique(x$case), w$case), ]
out <- data.frame(
  case = w$case,
  best_before = round(w$min_ms.before, 2),
  best_after = round(w$min_ms.after, 2),
  best_ratio = round(w$min_ms.after / w$min_ms.before, 2),
  median_before = round(w$median_ms.before, 2),
  median_after = round(w$median_ms.after, 2),
  median_ratio = round(w$median_ms.after / w$median_ms.before, 2)
)
print(out, row.names = FALSE)

Yes, I made the change, and it's a small but consistent win: about 3 to 7% faster on the best times for double arrays. I haven't committed it.

The change (src/extract.c and src/decl/extract-decl.h)

  • rray_location_offset_int/dbl() are gone. In their place, rray_location_is_missing_int() and rray_location_is_missing_dbl() return a bool.
  • RRAY_EXTRACT_LOCATIONS_LOOP now takes an IS_MISSING argument instead of OFFSET. The loop body is: 
    const INDEX_CTYPE location = v_index[i];
POKE(out, i, IS_MISSING(location) ? MISSING : v_x[(r_ssize) location - 1]);
  • The extract tests pass (102).

What the compiler does with it

I compiled both versions with -O2 and compared the generated code. The compiler had not already optimized away the second ternary, so the saving is real:

  • Int locations: 11 instructions per element before, 8 after.
  • Double locations: per element, sub, csinv, add, cmn, csel becomes add, csel. The - 1 is also moved out of the loop entirely.

Benchmark

Both builds were installed with R CMD INSTALL (-O2) and run alternately for 5 rounds each. The inputs are a 1e6-element x and 1e7 locations; the character cases use 1e6 locations. "Best" is the fastest run across all rounds, which is the most stable measure here.

Case Best before (ms) Best after (ms) Ratio
dbl x, random int 17.23 16.74 0.97
dbl x, random dbl 21.41 20.48 0.96
dbl x, random int, 10% NA 20.72 20.04 0.97
dbl x, random dbl, 10% NA 27.25 26.50 0.97
dbl x, sequential int 15.92 14.74 0.93
dbl x, sequential dbl 18.59 17.80 0.96
dbl x, sequential int, 10% NA 19.58 18.57 0.95
dbl x, sequential dbl, 10% NA 25.39 24.27 0.96
chr x, random int 4.50 4.23 0.94
chr x, sequential int 3.66 3.85 1.05
  • The machine is noisy. Both builds jump between about 20 ms and 26 ms from one round to the next, probably because macOS moves the process between fast and slow CPU cores. My first 3-round run looked like a 1.28x slowdown for one case because of this.
  • Medians agree with the best times. Every double-array case is 0 to 19% faster.
  • Character arrays show no clear change. The chr sequential case is 1.05 on best time but 0.94 on median. That path is dominated by r_chr_poke(), so the saved instructions matter less there.
  • Random order gains the least, about 3%, because those runs mostly wait on memory rather than on the loop itself.

One more thing I noticed in the machine code: r_globals.na_int is reloaded from memory on every loop iteration. The compiler can't prove the writes to v_out don't change it, so it can't skip the reload. Copying it into a local const before the loop might save a little more. I haven't tried it.

The benchmark script and both builds are in /tmp/rray4-bench/ if you want to rerun anything.

First I'll check which loops actually reload r_globals.na_int. The compiler only has to reload it where the store to out might overwrite it, so it may already be kept in a register for some output types.

Thinking

I found that the logical, integer, raw, and character loops re-read r_globals.na_int each iteration since the compiler can't rule out aliasing (unlike the double case, where it's already cached). I'll export HEAD to a scratch copy and edit just that loop to load na_int into a local once before iterating, to test without modifying the repo.

#define RRAY_EXTRACT_LOCATIONS_LOOP(                                           \
  POKE,                                                                        \
  MISSING,                                                                     \
  INDEX_CTYPE,                                                                 \
  INDEX_CONST_DEREF,                                                           \
  IS_MISSING                                                                   \
)                                                                              \
  do {                                                                         \
    const INDEX_CTYPE* v_index = INDEX_CONST_DEREF(subscript.index);           \
                                                                               \
    for (r_ssize i = 0; i < subscript.size; ++i) {                             \
      const INDEX_CTYPE location = v_index[i];                                 \
      POKE(                                                                    \
        out,                                                                   \
        i,                                                                     \
        IS_MISSING(location) ? MISSING : v_x[(r_ssize) location - 1]           \
      );                                                                       \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

Thinking

The benchmark will compare int-location writes across each atomic x type plus character x, alongside a double-location isnan() case as a control, since the current HEAD code recomputes addresses or reloads na_int differently per type.

Write /tmp/rray4-bench/bench2.R 
args <- commandArgs(trailingOnly = TRUE)
lib <- args[[1]]
label <- args[[2]]

library(rray4, lib.loc = lib)

set.seed(1)

n_x <- 1e6
n_i <- 1e7
dimensions <- c(100L, 100L, 100L)

x_int <- array(sample.int(1000L, n_x, TRUE), dimensions)
x_lgl <- array(sample(c(TRUE, FALSE), n_x, TRUE), dimensions)
x_dbl <- array(runif(n_x), dimensions)
x_cpl <- array(complex(real = runif(n_x), imaginary = runif(n_x)), dimensions)
x_raw <- array(as.raw(sample.int(255L, n_x, TRUE)), dimensions)
x_chr <- array(as.character(sample.int(1000L, n_x, TRUE)), dimensions)

random <- sample.int(n_x, n_i, replace = TRUE)
sequential <- rep_len(seq_len(n_x), n_i)

sequential_na <- sequential
sequential_na[sample.int(n_i, n_i %/% 10L)] <- NA

cases <- list(
  "int x, random int" = list(x_int, random),
  "int x, sequential int" = list(x_int, sequential),
  "int x, sequential int, 10% NA" = list(x_int, sequential_na),
  "lgl x, sequential int" = list(x_lgl, sequential),
  "raw x, sequential int" = list(x_raw, sequential),
  "cpl x, sequential int" = list(x_cpl, sequential),
  "dbl x, random int" = list(x_dbl, random),
  "dbl x, sequential int" = list(x_dbl, sequential),
  "chr x, sequential int (1e6)" = list(x_chr, sequential[seq_len(1e6)]),
  "control: dbl x, sequential dbl" = list(x_dbl, as.double(sequential))
)

out <- lapply(names(cases), function(name) {
  case <- cases[[name]]
  x <- case[[1]]
  i <- case[[2]]
  result <- bench::mark(
    rray_extract(x, i),
    min_iterations = 40,
    check = FALSE,
    filter_gc = FALSE
  )
  data.frame(
    build = label,
    case = name,
    min_ms = as.numeric(result$min) * 1000,
    median_ms = as.numeric(result$median) * 1000
  )
})

out <- do.call(rbind, out)
write.csv(out, stdout(), row.names = FALSE)

Bash: cd /tmp/rray4-bench && Rscript -e ' 
cd /tmp/rray4-bench && Rscript -e '
x <- read.csv("results3.csv", header = FALSE, col.names = c("round", "build", "case", "min_ms", "median_ms"))
best <- aggregate(min_ms ~ case + build, x, min)
med <- aggregate(median_ms ~ case + build, x, median)
w <- merge(
  reshape(best, idvar = "case", timevar = "build", direction = "wide"),
  reshape(med, idvar = "case", timevar = "build", direction = "wide")
)
w <- w[match(unique(x$case), w$case), ]
ratios <- reshape(x[c("round", "build", "case", "min_ms")], idvar = c("round", "case"), timevar = "build", direction = "wide")
ratios$r <- ratios$min_ms.hoisted / ratios$min_ms.head
rng <- aggregate(r ~ case, ratios, function(r) sprintf("%.2f-%.2f", min(r), max(r)))
out <- data.frame(
  case = w$case,
  best_head = round(w$min_ms.head, 2),
  best_hoisted = round(w$min_ms.hoisted, 2),
  best_ratio = round(w$min_ms.hoisted / w$min_ms.head, 2),
  per_round_ratio = rng$r[match(w$case, rng$case)],
  median_ratio = round(w$median_ms.hoisted / w$median_ms.head, 2)
)
print(out, row.names = FALSE)
'

Bash: git status --short && git log --oneline -1 
git status --short && git log --oneline -1
3e194ed Check extract locations for missingness instead of computing sentinel offsets

Mostly no. Reading na_int into a local before the loop saves nothing measurable for any type except raw x, which is about 6 to 8% faster. I'd skip it. The repo is unchanged: I tested it in a scratch copy of HEAD, and HEAD is still 3e194ed.

What I tried

  • The locations loop read r_globals.na_int once into a local, and rray_location_is_missing_int() compared against that local.
  • The machine code confirms the reload is gone. For integer x, the loop drops from 8 to 6 instructions per element. Double, complex and character x lose one or two instructions each as well.

Benchmark

1e7 int locations into a 1e6-element x (1e6 locations for character), 5 alternating rounds per build. "Best" is the fastest run across all rounds.

Case Best HEAD (ms) Best local copy (ms) Ratio Per-round ratios
int x, random 14.83 14.86 1.00 0.98 to 1.02
int x, sequential 13.12 13.29 1.01 1.01 to 1.02
int x, sequential, 10% NA 17.06 16.95 0.99 0.99 to 1.02
lgl x, sequential 13.30 13.17 0.99 0.97 to 1.01
raw x, sequential 9.58 8.99 0.94 0.91 to 0.98
cpl x, sequential 19.57 20.24 1.03 1.01 to 1.06
dbl x, random 17.56 17.29 0.98 0.95 to 1.00
dbl x, sequential 15.09 15.28 1.01 0.99 to 1.02
chr x, sequential 3.85 3.75 0.97 0.96 to 1.02
Control: dbl locations 18.28 18.44 1.01 0.99 to 1.02

Why it doesn't help

  • The reload always hits the CPU's fastest cache and runs alongside the rest of the loop. The real limit is fetching v_x[location - 1] and writing out, so two fewer instructions don't change the time.
  • Raw is the exception. Its loop does the least work per element, so the reload is a bigger share of it. Its result is the only one that holds in every round.

Why I'd skip it

The gain only shows up for raw x. To get it, both location helpers would have to take an na_int argument, and the double version would ignore it. That extra argument costs more in clarity than a speedup on raw arrays is worth.

The scratch copy, both builds and bench2.R are in /tmp/rray4-bench/ if you want to rerun anything.
